class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    //return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return 'counter
            return False

        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i - truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;

Let's implement:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def is_forbidden_prefix(s: str):
            t = trie
            for idx, c in enumerate(s, 1):
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return idx
            return False

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        j = n
        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = is_forbidden_prefix(word[i:j])
            if length:
                j = i + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)
        return ans
```

But wait, is `word[i:j]` slicing O(n) each iteration, leading O(n^2). Original code uses slicing. Need maybe optimize? The prompt says fix code, not necessarily optimize. But LeetCode constraints maybe require O(n * maxLen). Slicing word[i:j] creates string length up to n each time, O(n^2) worst-case if no forbidden. But forbidden list maybe large; but still n=1e5, O(n^2) too slow. Need consider if we should fix algorithm to avoid slicing? The faulty code uses slicing; maybe accepted? Let's recall LeetCode 2781 constraints: word.length <= 10^5, forbidden.length <= 10^5, forbidden[i].length <= 10. Need O(N * 10 * 26) maybe. Slicing word[i:j] each i could be O(N^2) in worst case if no forbidden and j=n, creating n-i length each iteration -> O(n^2) ~ 1e10, too slow. But maybe in Python, slicing is O(length), so TLE. ```
def forbidden_prefix_length(start, end):
    t = trie
    for pos in range(start, end):
        c = word[pos]
        if c not in t:
            return 0
        t = t[c]
        if "end" in t:
            return pos - start + 1
    return 0
```

def isForbidden(s):
    t = trie
    counter = 0
    for c in s:
        if c not in t:
            return False
        t = t[c]
        counter += 1
        if "end" in t:
            return counter
    return False

    truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
    if truc:
        j = i + truc - 1
    res = max(res, j - i)

Let's design final code:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        if max_len == 0:
            return len(word)

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        j = n

        def forbidden_prefix_len(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(end, start + max_len)
            for pos in range(start, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - start + 1
            return 0

        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_len(i, j)
            if length:
                j = i + length - 1
            if j - i > ans:
                ans = j - i
        return ans
```

Now, let's consider if the final code should be exactly:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        if max_len == 0:
            return len(word)

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        j = n

        def forbidden_prefix_len(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(end, start + max_len)
            for pos in range(start, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - start + 1
            return 0

        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_len(i, j)
            if length:
                j = i + length - 1
            if j - i > ans:
                ans = j - i

        return ans
```

    return 0 if forbidden else len(word)
```

Now, let's consider if there is a more efficient way to check forbidden prefixes using a set of forbidden strings and checking substrings of length up to max_len starting at i. The trie approach is fine.

Let's maybe think about if the reverse sliding window algorithm with trie is known. It is essentially:
```
n = len(word)
ans = 0
right = n
for left in range(n-1, -1, -1):
    node = trie
    for pos in range(left, right):
        if word[pos] not in node:
            break
        node = node[word[pos]]
        if "end" in node:
            right = pos
            break
    ans = max(ans, right-left)
```

Let's decide final code:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        if max_len == 0:
            return 0 if forbidden else len(word)

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        j = n

        def forbidden_prefix_len(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(end, start + max_len)
            for pos in range(start, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - start + 1
            return 0

        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_len(i, j)
            if length:
                j = i + length - 1
            if j - i > ans:
                ans = j - i

        return ans
```

Now, let's consider if the code should be exactly:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        if max_len == 0:
            return 0 if forbidden else len(word)

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        right = n

        def forbidden_prefix_len(left: int, right: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(right, left + max_len)
            for pos in range(left, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - left + 1
            return 0

        for left in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_len(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            if right - left > ans:
                ans = right - left

        return ans
```

This uses `right` variable instead of `j` for clarity. But original used `j`; either fine. The helper parameter `right` shadows outer `right`? In Python, parameter `right` is local; no assignment to outer `right`, so no issue. But using same name might be confusing. We can use `end` parameter. Let's use `end` to avoid shadowing.

```
def forbidden_prefix_len(start: int, end: int) -> int:
    ...
```

In main, `right` is outer. Good.

Let's decide final code with empty check:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        if any(f == "" for f in forbidden):
            return 0

        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        if max_len == 0:
            return len(word)

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        right = n

        def forbidden_prefix_len(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(end, start + max_len)
            for pos in range(start, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - start + 1
            return 0

        for left in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_len(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)

        return ans
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        if any(f == "" for f in forbidden):
            return 0

        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        if max_len == 0:
            return len(word)

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        right = n

        def forbidden_prefix_len(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(end, start + max_len)
            for pos in range(start, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - start + 1
            return 0

        for left in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_len(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)

        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        if any(f == "" for f in forbidden):
            return 0

        trie = {}
        max_len = 0
        for f in forbidden:
            max_len = max(max_len, len(f))
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        if max_len == 0:
            return len(word)

        n = len(word)
        ans = 0
        right = n

        def forbidden_prefix_len(start: int, end: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            limit = min(end, start + max_len)
            for pos in range(start, limit):
                c = word[pos]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                if "end" in t:
                    return pos - start + 1
            return 0

        for left in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_prefix_len(left, right)
            if length:
                right = left + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, right - left)

        return ans
